|**2026-11-09**|**Finite-sample Calibration Guarantees for Binary Segmentation**|Bruno Viti et.al.|Machine learning based segmentation models are frequently miscalibrated, producing for each pixel a label prediction whose associated confidence does not accurately reflect the true underlying probability. This limitation is particularly critical in high-stakes applications such as medical image segmentation. Although several calibration methods have been proposed, only a small subset of them provides theoretical guarantees on the calibration error, i.e., they do not typically ensure that, with high probability, the model exhibits a low calibration error. In this work, we develop a post-hoc calibration method for which formal calibration guarantees can be established. To achieve this, we derive from standard concentration inequalities an improved theoretical bound that explicitly accounts for the spatial dependence inherent in binary segmentation outputs. We empirically demonstrate that the proposed calibrator satisfies the derived guarantee and outperforms existing post-hoc calibration approaches.|[2611.09120](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09120)|
|**2026-11-09**|**CoMET: Tracking the Shared Pose Error of Roadside Messages for Asynchronous Vehicle-Infrastructure Cooperative 3D Detection**|Shuwei Sun et.al.|A roadside sensor can detect vehicles hidden from the ego vehicle, but a pose error shifts every detection in the same message. We study asynchronous vehicle-infrastructure cooperative 3D detection on V2X-Seq using only information available at decision time, without ground truth, future frames, or per-pair calibration offsets at inference. Objects seen by both sensors reveal the shared translation, but even at zero added delay, 38% of validation messages have no matched pair. We propose CoMET (Common-Mode Error Tracking), which estimates this translation over time. At each message arrival, CoMET matches vehicle and roadside detections, estimates the offset from mutually nearest pairs, and updates a Kalman filter for that sender. When no pair matches, the filter carries its estimate forward. The correction applies to all roadside boxes, including those unseen by the ego vehicle. The estimator has no learned parameters; a separate frozen head ranks the merged detections. Across four delays from 0 to 400 ms on 21 validation sequences, CoMET averages 66.63 bird's-eye-view AP at IoU 0.7, compared with 63.18 for the same late fusion using uncorrected online poses and 62.96 for the best intermediate-fusion baseline. Across five detectors from three architectures, CoMET gains 2.2 to 3.5 AP points over online poses and 0.3 to 0.6 over estimating the offset from the current message alone. We find that the benchmark uses a zero offset as a placeholder in 28% of validation messages and that its offset search reaches the boundary in 45% of the rest. These offsets affect the labels for objects seen only from the roadside. Under two alternative label references, CoMET improves on uncorrected online poses in every evaluated population. Retraining intermediate fusion with CoMET poses and corrected labels improves detection on all three architectures tested, with statistically significant gains on two.|[2611.09121](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09121)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Look Closer, Align Better: Explicit Visual Attributes for Robust EEG-to-Image Decoding**|Ke Xiao et.al.|EEG-to-image retrieval commonly aligns neural responses with representations from pretrained vision encoders. However, these representations may underrepresent fine-grained visual details needed to distinguish semantically similar images. To address this limitation, we first investigate the EEG decodability and complementary predictive value of explicit visual attributes, identifying visual cues that capture EEG-relevant information beyond pretrained representations. Building on these findings, we propose AttrEcho, a framework that enriches pretrained visual representations with complementary, spatially aligned visual attributes. Specifically, Region-Aligned Visual Attribute Fusion (RAVF) integrates EEG-relevant local visual attributes with spatially corresponding pretrained features through a learnable signed residual gate. We further employ Feature Token Aggregation (FTA), which learns dimension-specific weights across regions to aggregate refined visual features for contrastive alignment with EEG representations. Experiments on THINGS-EEG demonstrate that AttrEcho achieves 94.8% Top-1 and 99.7% Top-5 accuracy in intra-subject 200-way retrieval, while maintaining 82.1% Top-1 accuracy as the gallery expands to 1,600 images with same-concept distractors. AttrEcho also achieves 25.3% Top-1 and 53.0% Top-5 accuracy in inter-subject retrieval. These results highlight the importance of EEG-relevant visual attributes and spatially aligned fusion for fine-grained EEG-to-image retrieval.|[2611.09123](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09123)|
|**2026-11-09**|**TouchAnnotator: Multi-Source Tactile Supervision with Privileged-View Distillation**|Ao Li et.al.|Egocentric video captures human-object interaction at scale but rarely includes tactile labels for hidden contact and pressure. We introduce TouchAnnotator, an ego-only bimanual tactile annotator trained with multi-source tactile supervision and privileged-view distillation. A canonical hand field unifies glove pressure, geometry-derived human contact, and robot fingertip deformation from six datasets and three embodiments, while source-aware masks preserve valid targets. During training, an EMA teacher observes synchronized ego and wrist views and distills hand-local representations and tactile predictions into a student that uses only ego video and estimated pose at inference. Compact past-clip memory supplies temporal context. We evaluate on EgoTouch, zero-shot FPHA transfer, and 860 synchronized glove recordings. The model transfers to bare-hand contact and produces qualitatively coherent tactile responses across bare-hand, gloved-hand, and robot embodiments. On measured recordings, it improves temporal accuracy by 5.38 points and Volumetric IoU by 5.02 points under a shared protocol. These results show that heterogeneous human--robot supervision and privileged training views can turn egocentric video into dense tactile annotations.|[2611.09129](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09129)|
|**2026-11-09**|**DINO-Nucleus: Multi-Level Self-Supervised Features for Nucleus Instance Segmentation**|Mohammad Sarhangzadeh et.al.|Nucleus instance segmentation and counting are essential for quantitative microscopy, enabling reliable analysis of cell populations, tissue organization, and disease-related changes. However, accurately separating touching nuclei remains difficult, and many existing approaches rely on complex models or sensitive post-processing procedures. We present DINO-Nucleus, a parameter-efficient framework that leverages multi-level self-supervised DINOv2 representations for nucleus instance segmentation and counting. The model jointly predicts nucleus regions, pixel-wise instance flows, and explicit nucleus centers, while a consistency objective encourages the predicted flows to converge toward their corresponding centers. Multi-level transformer features provide complementary spatial and semantic information, and the pretrained backbone is adapted using lightweight LoRA modules to reduce fine-tuning cost. Experimental results show that DINO-Nucleus achieves strong segmentation and counting performance with parameter-efficient backbone adaptation. These findings demonstrate that parameter-efficient adaptation of multi-level self-supervised features can provide an effective solution for nucleus instance segmentation in microscopy images.|[2611.09133](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09133)|
|**2026-11-09**|**FIND: Frequency Invariance Disentanglement for Test-Time Adaptation in LiDAR 3D Detection**|Dongxiao Li et.al.|LiDAR-based 3D Object Detection (L3OD) is a fundamental 3D perception task, yet its performance is often compromised by domain shifts arising from diverse sensor configurations and environmental conditions. Existing Test-Time Adaptation (TTA) methods primarily leverage self-supervision in the spatial domain, yet they frequently confound semantic content with domain style due to the sparse, non-Euclidean nature of point clouds. To overcome this limitation, we propose FIND (Frequency INvariance Disentanglement), a novel TTA framework that precisely disentangles domain-invariant content from domain-specific interference within the frequency domain. Our methodology utilizes B-Spline fitting to construct locally adaptive filters integrated into a dual-stream architecture: an Invariant Stream extracts robust features to guide pseudo-labeling, while a Specific Stream enforces consistency against domain perturbations. By combining invariance-driven alignment with stability-guided regularization, our approach dynamically extracts robust domain-invariant features while suppressing domain-specific interference. Extensive experiments on cross-dataset adaptation and robust corruption benchmarks demonstrate that FIND significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods. The source code is publicly available at https://github.com/llldx03/FIND.|[2611.09170](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09170)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Bootstrap Your Own AV-Proxies: Adaptive Contrastive and Prototype Learning for Audio-Visual Segmentation**|Junbo Zhang et.al.|Audio-Visual Segmentation (AVS) aims to accurately segment sounding objects in video frames by leveraging audio-visual correspondence cues. However, it remains challenging due to the intrinsic semantic incompleteness within a single modality and the semantic gap between audio and visual representations. Traditional feature-fusion decoding approaches struggle to suppress fusion noise effectively, while recent methods that incorporate data-dependent priors often increase the complexity of modeling audio-visual correlations, leading to poor cross-domain generalization. To address these issues, we propose a novel adaptive contrastive and prototype learning framework, BYOAVP, for AVS. Specifically, we design a Self-Supervised Audio Enhancement (SSAE) module that introduces contrastive learning to adaptively align audio representations with gradient-blocked visual embeddings, thus narrowing the semantic gap between modalities. Furthermore, a Dynamic Prototype Constraint (DPC) module is developed to refine pixel-wise category perception via momentum-based prototype updating, while enhancing localization of sounding regions through cross-modal interaction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves SOTA performance across two AVS benchmarks and six sub-tasks, exhibiting strong robustness and generalization ability.|[2611.09191](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09191)|
|**2026-11-09**|**InstantHMR : Real-Time Human Mesh Recovery on Your Phone**|Mohamed Adjel (LAAS-GEPETTO) et.al.|Real-time 3D human mesh recovery from a single image on mobile devices is a challenging problem with many applications, including robotics, biomechanics, AR/VR, and exergames. Current state-of-the-art models are either too slow to run efficiently on mobile devices or sacrifice accuracy for speed. We present InstantHMR, a family of open-source single-image regressors suited for mobile inference, based on the Momentum Human Rig (MHR) parametric model and trained exclusively on publicly available annotations from the SAM 3D Body dataset. Our approach achieves remarkable performance on a Samsung Galaxy S23 NPU, recovering a 6,890-vertex body mesh in 5.5 ms per person, and 0.98 ms on an NVIDIA RTX 4070 GPU. This speed surpasses all existing methods we evaluated. On mobile, it is 12x faster than the single mobile-compatible method that slightly outperforms ours in accuracy, while achieving competitive accuracy with 40.0 mm PA-MPJPE on 3DPW, 48.8 mm on EMDB and 41.7 mm on RICH. To ensure fair comparisons, we re-evaluate 11 state-of-the-art methods under a unified protocol aligned with the closest MHR-based competitors, including Meta's official MHR-to-SMPL conversion, and report their reproduction results. Additionally, we measure latency across eight different targets, ranging from desktop TensorRT to two phone NPUs. Code and models are available at https://github.com/Naturalpad-studio/InstantHMR.|[2611.09195](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09195)|
|**2026-11-09**|**HMMF-Net: A Lightweight Hierarchical Multi-Frame Multimodal Fusion Network for Accurate Fish Feeding Intensity Assessment**|Phi-Hung Hoang et.al.|Automated fish feeding intensity assessment (FFIA) is important for precision aquaculture and feed cost optimization. However, unimodal methods are vulnerable to acoustic noise and visual disturbances, while existing multimodal frameworks often rely on computationally intensive 3D convolutions or Transformers, limiting their use on edge devices. To address these limitations, we propose HMMF-Net, a lightweight multimodal architecture that combines early multi-frame visual encoding and multi-axis acoustic modeling through a hierarchical bidirectional messenger cross-modal fusion (BMCF) mechanism. The visual branch captures temporal variations in feeding behavior by stacking temporally distant frames as a multi-channel input to an adapted 2D EfficientNetB0 backbone. The acoustic branch employs adaptive multi-axis residual units (AMARU) to extract temporal, frequency, and local time-frequency representations from log-mel spectrograms. BMCF progressively exchanges information between the two modalities at intermediate representation levels through compact messenger tokens, reducing the computational overhead of dense cross-modal attention. Evaluated on the AV-FFIA dataset using five-fold cross-validation, HMMF-Net achieves 96.77% accuracy and 96.88% F1-score on the held-out test folds, with 3.987M parameters and 1.002 GFLOPs. Compared with the best-performing visual configuration (95.49% accuracy), HMMF-Net reduces the classification error by 28.38%. Deployment on an NVIDIA Jetson Orin Nano using TensorRT with FP32 precision maintains comparable accuracy, with an inference time of 11.54 ms per 2-second sample and a model size of 18.81 MB. These results demonstrate that HMMF-Net combines effective multimodal representation learning with computational efficiency, enabling accurate and practical FFIA on resource-constrained edge platforms.|[2611.09224](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09224)|
|**2026-11-09**|**T2I Models Rarely Invert Negation but Often Fail to Suppress Negated Concepts: Benchmarking Negation Suppression in Text-to-Image Generation**|Akansha Gautam et.al.|Text-to-image diffusion models often fail to suppress explicitly negated concepts, such as a lamp for "a desk without a lamp." We introduce T2I-NSI, a benchmark and metric for measuring negation sensitivity in text-to-image generation. It pairs each negated prompt with a canonical positive counterpart and factorizes negation by linguistic form (lexical/semantic, morphological, syntactic) and target type (objects, attributes, relations, compositions, and nested cases). The benchmark contains 759,869 paired prompt atoms over a 5,082-object catalog, with 916,272 images (911,982 open-weights, 4,290 closed-source), scored by a Basic CLIP estimator and a Robust ensemble of CLIP, VQA and open-vocabulary detection. Negation sensitivity is low: open-weights generators score NSI <= 0.038, while GPT-Image-1.5 and Gemini-2.5-Flash-Image reach about 0.09. The dominant failure is under-suppression rather than misdirection. Inverse interpretation is essentially absent (0.00% across over 450K atom-model evaluations), whereas models render the concept under both prompts on 8-19% of atoms and only partly suppress it on a further 9-25%; most remaining atoms are too weakly rendered to label. A blinded human study (420 atoms, nine annotators) corroborates the near-absence of inversion and the high persistence rate, and a matched-prompt comparison shows the open-versus-closed gap (~3.5x) is not a prompt-generator artifact. Code and data: https://github.com/IAB-IITJ/T2I-NSI|[2611.09304](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09304)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Interpretable Context-Aware Multi-Label Retinal Classification with Dual-Hub Heterogeneous Graph Neural Network**|Proshanto Chanda et.al.|Multi-label retinal screening requires capturing co-occurring pathologies and clinical context, yet standard multimodal fusion remains opaque. We propose a dual-hub heterogeneous graph neural network (GNN) that couples spatial fundus features and structured clinical covariates through cross-modal message passing while providing inherent interpretability. Rather than relying on post-hoc saliency approximations, our architecture exposes multi-level decision pathways during forward aggregation: spatially biased self-attention captures lesion-level patch interactions, while modality-specific graph attention coefficients explicitly quantify the contribution of visual regions and clinical risk factors to each disease node. Evaluated on the BRSET dataset (16,266 photographs from 8,524 patients), the proposed model achieves 76.1% Macro-AUPRC across seven conditions, matching or exceeding image-only (75.9%) and ResNet-50+MLP fusion (75.9%) baselines, with largest contextual gains in macular edema (+3.5%) and diabetic retinopathy (+2.1%). Codebase is available at: https://github.com/proshanto-c/ca-mlc-refactor.|[2611.09309](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09309)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Keyline Detection with YOLO: Runway Perception for Vision-Based Aircraft Landing**|Leander Fritsch et.al.|Modern object detectors can predict keypoints as per-object geometric features, but keypoints are inherently vulnerable to occlusion and truncation. Object edges can provide complementary information, often remaining observable even when partially occluded or when their defining endpoints leave the field of view. We therefore introduce object keyline detection, the task of predicting a fixed, semantically ordered set of infinite lines per object instance. Unlike the outputs of general line-segment detectors, each keyline is associated with an object instance and identified by a semantic index, providing a line-level analog of keypoints. To solve this task, we extend YOLO with keyline prediction parameterized in continuous Hesse-normal form $[\rho,\cos\theta,\sin\theta]$, and introduce Object Keyline Similarity (OKLS), which serves as an evaluation metric, target assignment signal, and regression loss. We instantiate this formulation on runway perception in vision-based landing, a motivating example containing semantic linear features that remain partially observable throughout the approach. Experiments show that our representation achieves the highest detection accuracy among four evaluated line parameterizations, and keylines integrate directly into standard YOLO heads as additional output channels without degrading keypoint performance or adding meaningful latency.|[2611.09316](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09316)|
|**2026-11-09**|**RegNC-Mamba3: Shared Structural Feature Learning Using Non-Causal State Space Principles for Multi-Modal Image Registration**|Zhikang Li et.al.|The main challenge of multi-modal image registration is shared structural feature extraction with higher accuracy and lower computational complexity. Compared with a State Space Model (SSM), the State Space Principles (SSP) formulation of Mamba-3 uses a more accurate discretization with lower truncation error while maintaining linear complexity. However, vanilla SSP is designed for natural language processing (NLP) and is not directly suitable for images. To address this issue, we propose a novel non-causal SSP (NC-SSP) with shared structure awareness and develop a coarse-to-fine registration method named RegNC-Mamba3. First, NC-SSP is designed to eliminate the dependence of the integral computation on the scanning path. By combining relative-weight normalization with aggregation over all circular scan paths, NC-SSP constructs a globally hidden state without long-range decay, which provides a wider receptive field and higher efficiency than the original Mamba-3. Second, NC-SSP is modified to strengthen the extraction of cross-modal structural features. When computing hidden states and feature interactions, we construct complex-valued sequences in NC-SSP by applying learnable Log-Gabor kernels, with different orientations to each head for frequency-domain modulation. This improves the perception of shared structural information across modalities and increases both registration accuracy and quality. Experiments on VIS-SAR and VIS-IR demonstrate that RegNC-Mamba3 outperforms the compared detector-based and semi-dense multi-modal registration methods.|[2611.09336](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09336)|
|**2026-11-09**|**HiMEM: Hippocampus-Inspired Memory Consolidation, Pruning, and Composition for Vision-Language-Action Models in 3D Scenes**|Boran Zhao et.al.|Memory plays a crucial role in long-horizon task execution and cross-task generalization for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models operating in complex 3D scenes. However, existing VLA memory mechanisms suffer from memory noise, particularly in complex 3D scenes, as stored episodic memories contain extensive redundant details about scene layouts, object configurations, and robot-specific states, which obscure generalizable manipulation principles and hinder cross-task transfer. To address this challenge, we first introduce a hippocampus-inspired memory consolidation mechanism that synthesizes extensive episodic experiences into a compact set of essential semantic memories, enabling VLA models to derive generalizable principles from practical experience. Second, we propose an episodic memory pruning mechanism that removes redundant episodic memories whose contents are captured by consolidated semantic memories, thereby increasing effective memory capacity and improving long-horizon task execution. Finally, to handle complex task scenarios, we develop a flexible memory composition mechanism that jointly leverages multiple episodic and semantic memories to enhance cross-scenario generalization. Experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus demonstrate the effectiveness of HiMEM across different VLA backbones, improving the average success rate of pi_0 from 91.88% to 98.20% on LIBERO.|[2611.09355](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09355)|
|**2026-11-09**|**SPSC: Zero-Shot Cross-Subject EEG-to-Image Reconstruction via Semantic Preservation and Subspace Calibration**|Yutang Lu et.al.|Pretrained vision-language models and diffusion generators have driven rapid progress in brain-to-image generation, while EEG-based reconstruction is still constrained by low signal-to-noise ratio and cross-subject semantic shifts. Zero-shot cross-subject reconstruction poses even greater challenges: EEG semantic embeddings learned from source subjects deviate from the manifold of image embeddings of the unseen target subject, particularly when neither target labels nor target image embeddings are available. To address this issue, we introduce the Semantic Preservation and Subspace Calibration (SPSC) framework, which first uses Object Mask Contrastive Regularization (OMCR) with object identity for backend training, and then constructs Subspace Candidate Gate (SCG) to achieve label-free semantic correction for target subjects during inference. Specifically, OMCR regularizes the conditioning space with object-level positive samples to preserve object-level feature neighborhoods within the reconstruction prior and low-level branch, while SCG exploits training image prototypes and batch statistics of unlabeled target EEG to select candidates for each task within subspaces of image prototypes. Under the leave-one-subject-out (LOSO) setting across ten subjects on the THINGS-EEG dataset, SPSC improves the cross-subject reconstruction from 0.552 to 0.713 on CLIP and from 0.612 to 0.745 on Alex.5. These significant gains indicate that the semantically calibrated conditions can better support generation for the target subjects under label-free inference.|[2611.09380](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09380)|
|**2026-11-09**|**A Bridge from Paste to Place: Physics-Guided One-Step Image Composition via Object-Scene Interaction**|Hongzhen Li et.al.|Realistic image composition must make a pasted object appear naturally present in a scene while preserving the fine details of both foreground and background. Reference-based insertion methods can change object pose and appearance, while foreground harmonization methods and shadow-specific synthesis methods each address only part of this task. An efficient, unified solution to this task remains an open challenge. We introduce Paste2Place, an end-to-end, physics-guided flow-based model that learns a direct, one-step latent bridge from pasted inputs to realistic composites, guided by explicit albedo-shading-residual decomposition and recomposition within the model. To support model training and evaluation, we develop a Blender rendering and validation pipeline to generate paired composites with physical annotations, and construct real-scene pairs from open-source object-removal data. This effort yields 86,273 training pairs and a 1,500-example benchmark. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that Paste2Place achieves substantial advantages in image composition quality and inference speed. Overall, our work provides a clear task definition and an effective solution for image composition, and fills a gap in large-scale, high-quality datasets and benchmarks for training and evaluating image composition.|[2611.09387](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09387)|
|**2026-11-09**|**The Role Of Recurrent Refinement In Medical Image Segmentation**|Joao D. S. Marques et.al.|While feed-forward segmentation networks perform well on large cohorts, they degrade in constrained clinical settings with few training samples and imaging artifacts. Human vision, by contrast, remains robust under these conditions, a property partly attributed to the recurrent and feedback connectivity of the visual cortex, which refines visual information over time. Inspired by this, we append lightweight recurrent refinement blocks to segmentation backbones, namely Conditional Random Fields (CRFs), modern Hopfield networks and convolutional GRUs, and study their behaviour as the number of iterations increases at test time (including beyond the training horizon). Evaluated on two open-source datasets, Kvasir- SEG and ARCADE, our recurrent blocks add less than 1.5% parameters and yield gains on clean data (up to 1.4 pp) and under noise (up to 17 pp) while reducing HD95. Code is available at https://github.com/joaodsmarques/recurrencehelpsegmentation .|[2611.09436](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09436)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Overview of the ImageCLEFmedical 2026 GANs task: Exploring fingerprint detection, privacy leakage, and synthetic medical image generation**|Alexandra-Georgiana Andrei et.al.|The 2026 ImageCLEFmedical GANs Task Exploring Fingerprint Detection, Privacy Leakage, and Synthetic Medical Image Generation continues to investigate privacy and security concerns around using patient data to generate synthetic medical images, now in its fourth edition. This edition introduces three complementary subtasks: the first extends prior editions by asking participants to detect which real images were used in training a Generative Adversarial Network to produce given synthetic outputs; the second is a novel subtask requiring participants to identify which latent vector produced each synthetic image, probing the faithfulness and reversibility of the latent-to-image mapping; the third asks participants to generate privacy-preserving CT slices, quantifying the trade-off between image fidelity and patient privacy. Ground-truth annotations and benchmark datasets of real and GAN-generated lung CT slices are provided for all subtasks. Evaluation is based on Cohen's Kappa for Subtask 1, matching accuracy for Subtask 2, and a combination of FID and Privacy Preservation Score (PPS) for Subtask 3. Overall, 37 teams registered for the task, with 10 teams submitting runs across subtasks and 6 teams submitting working notes papers. This paper presents an overview of the task setup, datasets, evaluation metrics, and summarises the approaches and results of participating teams.|[2611.09455](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09455)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Left-Right-Consistent Iterative Stereo Matching via Cross-Injection**|Manuel Knott et.al.|State-of-the-art stereo matching models predominantly predict the disparity of only the left (reference) view. However, applications such as per-eye AR/VR rendering, novel view synthesis, or two-view depth fusion require disparity for both views, and need those two maps to agree. The usual workaround of running the network a second time on the horizontally flipped and swapped pair gives two independent predictions that can be inconsistent, especially on transparent surfaces, in textureless regions, and at occlusion boundaries. We introduce cross-injection (CI), a lightweight extension for iterative (refinement-based) models that can be added to any pretrained left-only iterative matcher. It couples the two passes by injecting the explicit, signed left-right inconsistency as an additional input cue at every step of the refinement stage. We empirically show that CI substantially reduces left-right disagreement on every benchmark (more than halving it on Booster for FoundationStereo), while largely preserving sharp object boundaries. Furthermore, using two state-of-the-art stereo matching models, we show that CI also reduces the single-view disparity error, making it a worthwhile extension even if only left disparity is needed.|[2611.09458](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09458)|
|**2026-11-09**|**FusionCLIP: Multimodal Contrastive Pretraining for Fusion Plasma Diagnostics**|Qiang Chen et.al.|Visible-light videos and multivariate diagnostic signals provide complementary observations of tokamak discharges. However, existing fusion pretraining methods, primarily learn representations by reconstructing or predicting observations, leaving the correspondence between these modalities underexplored. Representation learning is further challenged by the fact that plasma-related regions occupy only part of each video frame and diagnostic variables have substantially different sampling rates. We propose FusionCLIP, a multimodal contrastive pretraining framework. Spatiotemporal Stability Selection retains informative visual tokens, while a Temporal Query Encoder integrates asynchronous diagnostic signals using their observation timestamps. Contrastive learning then aligns the two modality representations. We construct EAST Multimodal Diagnostics (EAST-MMD), comprising 1307 EAST discharges, and evaluate the model on three independently fine-tuned tasks: disruption prediction, edge-localized mode (ELM) classification, and $H_{98}$ regression. FusionCLIP achieves an aggregate score of 89.07\%, exceeding the best general-purpose pretraining configuration and an in-domain contrastive pretraining configuration without Spatiotemporal Stability Selection by 6.27 and 2.12 percentage points, respectively. Ablations and attention visualizations further support the contributions of its key components.|[2611.09493](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09493)|
|**2026-11-09**|**BEiTScore++: Reference-free Image Captioning Evaluation with BEiT-3 and Conformalized Quantile Regression**|Gon\c{c}alo Gomes et.al.|Reliable uncertainty estimation is important for deploying evaluation metrics in real-world settings, where both input ambiguity and domain shifts are common. In this work, we propose an uncertainty-aware extension of the BEiTScore image captioning metric, producing conformally calibrated confidence intervals alongside alignment scores. Our approach models predictive distributions over human judgments, enabling both accurate scoring and principled uncertainty quantification in a single forward pass. We evaluate our BEiTScore++ metric across multiple benchmarks with human-annotated ratings, showing that it preserves the ranking correlation of BEiTScore, while producing intervals whose width tracks the prediction error more closely than Monte Carlo dropout on most benchmarks. The estimated uncertainty also grows under image degradation and on out-of-domain artwork images, making BEiTScore++ a practical tool for uncertainty-aware evaluation of image-caption alignment.|[2611.09505](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09505)|
|**2026-11-09**|**V-STORM: A Structure-Aware Grounded Chain of Thought Dataset for Faithful and Fine-Grained Spatio-Temporal Video Reasoning**|Dian Jiao et.al.|While MLLMs can often identify what happens in a video, they still struggle with fine-grained spatio-temporal reasoning, which requires precise grounding when and where the supporting evidence appears and composing such evidence into a coherent reasoning chain. This challenge is exacerbated by the lack of scalable data construction and evaluation pipelines that provide granular, verifiable supervision linking reasoning steps to explicit spatio-temporal evidence.To address this gap, we present V-STORM, a structure-aware framework that transforms videos into Spatio-Temporal Scene Graphs (STSGs) and derives Grounded Chain of Thought (GCoT) from validated evidence paths, making each reasoning step traceable to structured and verifiable spatio-temporal evidence.Under this framework, we construct a 260K-sample training set and a 3K-sample human-verified benchmark, both featuring GCoT annotations that link reasoning to explicit spatio-temporal evidence.Experiments reveal a clear separation between evidence localization and evidence composition: current MLLMs can often identify relevant spatio-temporal cues, yet still fail to integrate them into correct multi-step reasoning, exposing a bottleneck that answer-only evaluation largely obscures. Moreover, fine-tuning with V-STORM substantially improves both answer accuracy and grounding faithfulness, validating its effectiveness as supervision for faithful and evidence-grounded video reasoning. Our dataset, benchmark, and source code will be publicly released.|[2611.09511](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09511)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Embedding Predictive Adaptation and Refinement for Multi-Modal Industrial Anomaly Detection**|Jaeho Cho et.al.|Industrial Anomaly Detection (IAD) has evolved from 2D image-based approaches to multi-modal methods that leverage the complementary nature of RGB images and 3D point clouds. Among these, memory bank-based methods, which identify anomalies by the discrepancy between each query patch feature and its nearest normal prototype, have proven particularly effective. However, two limitations remain: (i) employing frozen pretrained encoders yields insufficient discrimination between normal and anomalous features due to the domain gap, while existing contrastive learning-based adaptation struggles to capture the spatial relationships required for precise localization; and (ii) pixel-level anomaly maps interpolated from patch-level distance scores remain coarse, lacking fine-grained detail. To address these limitations, we propose EPAR (Embedding Predictive Adaptation and Refinement). We adapt encoders by predicting the embeddings of masked regions to capture spatial relationships, together with patch-level Variance-Invariance-Covariance (VIC) regularization, resolving the poor discriminability between normal and anomalous features caused by the domain gap. We further propose a Noise Suppression Network that refines coarse maps by learning the relational discrepancy between each patch feature and its nearest normal prototype, rather than the anomaly's appearance. This relational signal remains valid for unseen real defects, despite being learned from synthetic anomalies. EPAR demonstrates strong practical applicability, excelling in few-shot settings and improving AUPRO@1% on MVTec 3D-AD and Eyecandies by +1.8% and +1.9%, respectively.|[2611.09521](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09521)|
|**2026-11-09**|**RadarDINO: Distilling a Visual Foundation Model into mmWave Radar for Human Mesh Reconstruction**|Furkan Mert Algan et.al.|Millimeter-wave (mmWave) radar offers a privacy-preserving and illumination-robust alternative for human mesh reconstruction (HMR). However, high-fidelity radar HMR remains challenging. Sparse point cloud-based methods suffer from information loss and missed detections, while raw radar tensor methods like RT-Mesh lack fine-grained semantics and explicit local body modeling. In this paper, we propose RadarDINO, a radar HMR framework that distills visual semantics from a DINOv3 teacher into 3D radar representations and introduces explicit geometric supervision. DINOv3 descriptors are transferred to radar voxels via calibrated RGB-radar geometry, providing voxel- and body-part-level supervision for part-aware radar representations. We further incorporate vertex- and joint-losses to directly constrain reconstructed geometry. On the M4Human dataset, our method outperforms the SOTA baseline across all three evaluation splits.|[2611.09524](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09524)|
|**2026-11-09**|**ExQuery: Explicit Structured Query Priors for Temporal 3D Object Detection**|Honglin Sun et.al.|Sparse query-based 3D detectors commonly encode heterogeneous semantic, geometric, and motion information into unified latent queries, including when explicit 3D anchors are used. We introduce ExQuery, an explicit structured query framework for temporal 3D object detection. Each decoded query is organized into a shared semantic context slice and physical-state-specific slices for center, dimensions, orientation, and velocity. This structure is preserved from prior encoding to branch-specific regression and is reused to reconstruct temporal queries: predicted physical states are transformed by ego motion, classification scores form soft semantic priors, and the reconstructed query is fused with the stored latent instance feature through gated affine modulation. Experiments on the nuScenes benchmark show that our method achieves competitive detection performance, and ablation studies validate the effectiveness of our proposed modules.|[2611.09527](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09527)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Multimodal Training with Knowledge Traction for Video Anomaly Detection**|Xiao Tan et.al.|The task of weakly supervised video anomaly detection is challenging. Existing approaches mainly model video frame features and determine whether anomalies are present based on their magnitudes. In this work, we propose a novel method that incorporates external knowledge into training to improve model performance, robustness, and generalization. We leverage textual prompts and a large language model (LLM) to establish prior information for the specific task, and employ dictionary learning to normalize and compress this information. Moreover, during experiments, we observed inconsistencies in the textual descriptions extracted from similar video frames by image caption models. To address this, we devise a method that leverages an LLM and auxiliary information to ensure consistency in the textual descriptions of similar frames within a video. Finally, we introduce a feature processing approach that incorporates prior knowledge and cross-modal attention mechanisms. This approach learns the relationships and characteristics among different modalities to construct more accurate and reliable feature representations, thereby improving the accuracy and stability of downstream tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that our method surpasses other state-of-the-art approaches.|[2611.09536](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09536)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Video Stitching from Multiple Moving Cameras**|Shira Ifergane et.al.|We address geometric video alignment for the same scene captured by multiple freely moving cameras. Existing methods, which are based on image stitching or video stabilization, are typically limited to two cameras or must assume the restrictive setting of a fixed linear ordering of the cameras. We propose MViS, a lightweight test-time optimization framework for joint multi-view alignment with temporal consistency. MViS uses a spatial-transformer-inspired net to jointly process frames from all views within a short temporal window and predict per-view, per-time homographies. Optimization proceeds in two stages: geometry-guided alignment followed by appearance-based refinement, with both stages enforcing multi-view and temporal consistency across the sequence. The method requires no scene-specific hyperparameter tuning, handles arbitrary and time-varying view-intersection graphs, and remains robust under rapid camera motion. On a standard two-view video benchmark, MViS matches or surpasses the state of the art. To facilitate evaluation in more complex scenarios, we introduce an annotated dataset with more than two freely moving cameras and establish the first benchmark for this setting. Project page: https://bgu-cs-vil.github.io/MViS-project/ .|[2611.09570](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09570)|
|**2026-11-09**|**MSR-ABC: Complementary Feature Fusion for Clear Cell Renal Cell Carcinoma Classification**|Yuan Liang et.al.|Clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC) classification from computed tomography (CT) may benefit from representations that capture different aspects of tumour phenotype. However, simply concatenating reconstruction-derived features, image--text embeddings, and radiomic descriptors may not fully exploit their complementary information. We propose MSR-ABC, a global--local framework integrating MedVAE, MedSigLIP, and radiomics. Spatially matched local image descriptors interact through cross-representation attention, while radiomic auxiliary supervision encourages the pooled local representation to retain quantitative tumour attributes. A gated residual mechanism integrates local information with global summaries. Area-aware pooling and visual and soft-text prompting complete the framework. We evaluate discrimination using a patient-level split of 396 KiTS23 cases and an external cohort of 111 TCGA/TCIA cases, reporting mean AUROC over three training seeds with 95% patient-bootstrap confidence intervals. MSR-ABC achieves mean AUROCs of 0.806 internally and 0.879 externally. Internally, it exceeds global MedVAE--radiomics and three-source concatenation by 3.35 and 3.61 AUROC percentage points, respectively. Removing any source lowers mean AUROC in both cohorts, with the largest decreases following radiomics removal. Individual removal of cross-representation attention, auxiliary supervision, or gated fusion also lowers internal mean AUROC. These findings are consistent with complementary contributions within the integrated architecture and support further investigation of structured representation fusion for non-invasive renal tumour characterisation.|[2611.09583](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09583)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Dual-Region Slice Aggregation for CT-Based Renal Tumor Classification**|Yuan Liang et.al.|Clear cell renal cell carcinoma (ccRCC) is the most common kidney cancer in adults. We present a region-aware architecture for CT-based ccRCC classification. Tumor and 0--10 mm peritumoral context are pooled separately from shared frozen DINOv3 feature maps. A shared position-aware slice aggregator precedes fixed training-only principal component analysis (PCA). The resulting representations are fused with radiomics using two scalar feature gates. Compared with matched single-tumor readout, mean AUC increases by 1.50 and 2.34 percentage points, respectively. These findings highlight the joint role of regional readout and slice aggregation in adapting frozen visual features to renal CT.|[2611.09599](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09599)|
|**2026-11-09**|**CRTrack-Cap: A New Benchmark for Cross-View Dense Video Object Captioning**|Xiangqun Zhang et.al.|Dense video object captioning (DVOC) localizes, tracks, and describes object trajectories within a video. Existing methods process each camera view independently, producing separate camera-local tracklets and captions for the same person across cameras. These outputs lack a unified description and leave complementary appearance evidence across views unused. We therefore formulate cross-view DVOC (CVDVOC), which associates these tracklets into scene-level global trajectories and generates one caption for each trajectory from synchronized views. To support this task, we construct CRTrack-Cap from CRTrack by auditing text-identity links and converting usable referring expressions into canonical captions. It retains 425 global trajectories and 838 local tracklets across 13 scenes, with canonical captions for 335 identities. We adapt CHOTA to the cross-view setting as CV-CHOTA to jointly evaluate tracking and caption quality. Our reference framework combines View-Exclusive Cross-Camera Association (VECA) for grouping tracklets with Conflict-Evidence Gating (CEG) for integrating caption evidence using its reliability and disagreement across views. Against three single-view methods adapted with MvMHAT for cross-view association, our framework achieves CV-CHOTA scores of 83.43 and 33.48 on the scene-disjoint in-domain and cross-domain tests, respectively. Together, CRTrack-Cap and our framework provide a benchmark and a strong baseline for describing the same person consistently across camera views.|[2611.09617](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09617)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Breaking the Privacy Promise: Gradient Inversion Attacks on Federated Low-Rank Adapted Vision Transformers**|Ritik K. Badiya et.al.|Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) has been increasingly adopted to fine-tune pretrained models with low computational cost and is therefore also preferred in the federated learning paradigm to reduce computational and communication overhead. In this work, we investigate the sparsely explored problem of the risk of private data leakage in LoRA-adapted Vision Transformers (ViTs), where low-rank updates weaken the gradient signal available to a server with access to individual client updates. We show that quality of reconstruction is limited by two coupled ambiguities: reduced information in LoRA gradients and missing spatial cues from frozen positional embeddings. To address this, we propose a spatially anchored generative inversion framework that incorporates server-prescribed trainable positional embeddings into ViT blocks during fine-tuning at clients. The resulting positional gradients act as layer-wise spatial anchors, providing additional positional cues, while the generative prior keeps reconstructions on a realistic image manifold, reducing duplicate-data and patch-permutation artifacts, {which produce similar gradients as those produced when the target client images are used}. Adding our proposed spatial anchoring to GIAS improves PSNR by 10.14 dB, SSIM by 0.45, and LPIPS by 0.098 on CIFAR-10, as well as PSNR gains of 7.83 and 11.74 dB on high-resolution CelebA and CARS datasets, respectively, in the FedSGD setting. These results indicate that LoRA-based PEFT does not, by itself, prevent data leakage in FL, and carefully incorporated positional embeddings can help in exposing private information.|[2611.09652](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09652)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Decoupled Mode Connectivity for Base-to-Novel Generalization in Vision-Language Models**|Imad Eddine Marouf et.al.|Prompt learning adapts vision-language models such as CLIP by optimizing a short learnable prompt. The cross-entropy objective pulls this prompt toward base-class specialization, whereas generalization to unseen classes benefits from staying close to CLIP's zero-shot feature space. Because both objectives act on the same prompt, their gradients conflict at convergence, and in our experiments no single-prompt loss design clearly improves on the cosine baseline. We propose Decoupled Mode Connectivity (DMC), which gives each objective its own prompt. Starting from a base-trained prompt, DMC trains a specialization prompt and a generalization prompt, and merges their text features with a fixed coefficient at test time, at the inference cost of a single prompt. A linear mode connectivity (LMC) corridor in text-feature space keeps the classification loss low for every classifier interpolated between the two endpoints, and a Visual Anchor KL term preserves CLIP's zero-shot class-similarity distribution of each image during specialization. We further argue that such regularizers should satisfy class-permutation invariance (CPI), and show via Fano's inequality, in an idealized model, that violating CPI lower-bounds the drop in novel accuracy by a term proportional to the mutual information between the prompt and the base-class labels. Across 11 datasets, DMC improves the average harmonic mean of base and novel accuracy over MergeTune for both CoOp and KgCoOp, and its interpolation curves pass beyond the single-prompt operating point, shifting the base-novel frontier rather than trading along it. Code is available at https://github.com/IemProg/DMC.|[2611.09655](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09655)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Video2Asset: Online Construction of Persistent 3D Street-Scene Assets**|Zihan Zheng et.al.|Online 3D street-scene asset construction turns multi-view video into a continually updated scene of independently editable objects. The central challenge is to accumulate partial, intermittent observations under motion and occlusion without fragmenting identities or mixing evidence across objects. To address this challenge, we propose Video2Asset, an online framework for constructing persistent 3D assets from multi-view video. Using occupancy as an intermediate representation, we construct identity-aware observations with Historical Carrier Guidance for instance decoding and an identity encoder fusing instance-aligned 3D features with multi-view appearance. Accumulated-Evidence Association compares current observations with identity, geometric, and appearance evidence accumulated within asset records to determine identity inheritance and update world-coordinate states. We instantiate meshes from an asset library using these states and combine them with an occupancy-derived static background to support scene visualization, mesh replacement, object deletion, and trajectory editing. Experiments on several datasets demonstrate improvements in temporal identity consistency and asset quality across multiple occupancy models, including a 15.4% reduction in asset fragmentation on nuScenes with SGE.|[2611.09661](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09661)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Dual Architecture Fusion Vehicle Lidar 3D Target Detection**|Muyun Li et.al.|In this paper, a 3D backbone network (SpconvTransformer) based on the fusion of convolutional architecture branch and Transformer architecture branch is proposed. The convolution branch uses 3D sparse convolution and submanifold convolution to learn local voxel features and semantic information, and the Transformer branch uses the self-attention mechanism to model the remote relationship between voxels, uses the broad receptive field to capture rich context information, and complementing the advantages of the two architectures by fusing the local features extracted from the two branches and the global features solves the problem of limited receptive fields of 3D convolutional neural networks. The experimental results show that the average accuracy of SpconvTransformer in 3D object detection and bird's-eye view detection tasks reaches 81.45% and 88.09%, respectively, and the average directional similarity reaches 91.10%, which is significantly better than other existing algorithms.|[2611.09664](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09664)|
|**2026-11-09**|**PoseRieFlow: Riemannian Flow Matching for Whole-Body Text-to-Pose Generation**|Jan Spindler et.al.|We present PoseRieFlow, a compact flow-matching framework for text-conditioned generation of whole-body 3D human poses. Prior text-to-pose methods either neglect articulated hands or model rotations in Euclidean space despite their non-Euclidean geometry. PoseRieFlow instead formulates generation directly on the product manifold of SMPL-H joint rotations, learning a text-conditioned vector field that transports samples from an isotropic $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ Gaussian prior to the data distribution along Riemannian geodesics. A part-tokenized Diffusion Transformer aggregates the 52 joints into eight body-part tokens, allocating a dedicated token and nonlinear projector to each hand, and restores per-joint resolution through part-specific decoders, shortening the sequence while improving kinematic fidelity over per-joint tokenization. With only 21.6M trainable parameters, PoseRieFlow supports expressive SMPL-H generation with detailed hand articulation at a fraction of the scale of recent LLM-based generators. On PoseScript, it attains the lowest whole-body kinematic error among all hand-producing methods, with consistent kinematic gains from the Riemannian formulation over its Euclidean counterpart at every practical inference budget, while distributional and retrieval metrics remain comparable between them.|[2611.09692](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09692)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Direct Regression and Geometric Recovery for Event-based Spacecraft Pose Estimation**|Pruthvi Nandan Janga et.al.|Event cameras offer high temporal resolution and dynamic range for uncooperative spacecraft pose estimation, but models trained on synthetic event streams must generalize to the noise, background activity, and contrast variation of physical sensors. We present a comparative systems study of two independently developed approaches to synthetic-to-real, event-based 6-DoF spacecraft pose estimation. The first is a modular pipeline that combines learned keypoint detection and unlabeled target-domain adaptation with geometry-based pose recovery and temporal smoothing. The second directly regresses translation and rotation from global and cropped event representations using an apparent-scale cue, physics-motivated synthetic corruptions, and feature-consistency regularization. We evaluate both systems under the same SPARK 2026 hidden-test protocol on the real-event split of SPADES, with predictions for all test frames. The results reveal a component-wise reversal concealed by the aggregate challenge score: direct regression reduces orientation error by 22.7% (1.1767 to 0.9101 rad) and obtains a 13.4% lower composite score, whereas the modular pipeline reduces relative translation error by 78.7% (0.1335 to 0.0285). These findings motivate a testable hybrid design that combines direct orientation estimation with geometry-constrained translation recovery.|[2611.09717](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09717)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Evaluating Anterior Cruciate Ligament Risk of Injury Using a Graph Convolutional Autoencoder**|Athanasios Babouras et.al.|Current solutions for assessing the risk of anterior cruciate ligament (ACL) injury typically rely on expensive motion-capture labs that require substantial resource investment or subjective evaluations of specific athletic stress tests by experts. In this paper, we develop an unsupervised learning model that runs entirely on a smartphone for two tasks, based on an autoencoder architecture with multiple layers of graph convolutional networks, which utilizes pose estimation data to determine a person's injury risk status. Our model accurately predicts athletes at high risk of injury for both athletic stress tests, with average sensitivities and specificities of 78% and 78% for the DVJ, and 75% and 74% for the SLH. Our findings support the development of a widely accessible smartphone solution for real-time screening of ACL injury risk.|[2611.09724](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09724)|
|**2026-11-09**|**TriNetra: Thermal-Reliability-Guided RGB-Thermal Fusion and Tracking for Surveillance**|Sadique Khatib et.al.|Visible cameras fail at night and thermal sensors fail under saturation, blur or loss of contrast, yet most RGB-thermal (RGB-T) detectors mix the two streams with fixed weights and cannot tell when one modality has become unreliable. TriNetra is a reliability-aware surveillance framework built around this problem. The proposed solution computes a frame-level Thermal Reliability Coefficient (TRC) from five label-free image cues, uses it to gate the thermal features of a dual-branch YOLOv8m detector whose visible branch, neck and head are frozen and COCO-pretrained, and passes the same score to a ByteTrack-based tracker that feeds a rule-based behaviour analyser. On the M3FD benchmark (2,940/630/630 split) the detector reaches 87.6% precision, 79.4% recall, 70.0% mAP@50 and 47.8% mAP@50:95 on the test split, with 17.0 M of its 42.9 M parameters trained. TRC stays near 0.78 on clean frames (standard deviation 0.012), decreases with the severity of thermal saturation and blur, reaching 0.53 and 0.69 at full severity, and is insensitive to RGB-thermal misalignment. With identical weights, the adaptive gate matches a fixed gate on clean data (70.0% versus 69.8% mAP@50) but keeps 43.6% mAP@50 when the thermal stream is corrupted, against 38.4%, 29.2% and 25.0% for a fixed gate, full thermal admission and a closed gate; the 5.1-point gain over the fixed gate has a 95% bootstrap confidence interval of +3.6 to +6.5 points. |**2026-11-09**|**Zero-Shot Benchmarking of Unified Multimodal Models for Shallow-Water UAV Image Distortion Assessment and Restoration**|Dario Scilla et.al.|Shallow-water UAV imagery provides centimeter-scale observations of benthic environments, but water-induced distortions complicate interpretation. To our knowledge, we present the first systematic zero-shot benchmark of unified multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for both assessing water-induced distortions and restoring shallow-water imagery within the same model framework. We evaluate five open-weight unified MLLMs with frozen pretrained weights and without access to paired reference images or descriptions of the true seabed. The benchmark contains 1,000 image pairs: 600 semi-synthetic UAV scenes created by applying synthetic water distortions to real shallow-water imagery and 400 fully synthetic through-water scenes. Three independent human annotations cover five distortion types. We evaluate four assessment prompts (P0-P3), from direct instruction to structured task decomposition, on all 1,000 images and four diagnosis-guided restoration prompts (R0-R3) on a common 100-image subset. Assessment varies substantially by model and prompt, with systematic prediction biases and a best pooled balanced accuracy of 0.654 for distortion presence. The best pooled PSNR gain is +0.255 dB, no unified MLLM improves pooled LPIPS, and some outputs alter scene structure or hallucinate content. Two restoration-only baselines also show limited transfer: InstructIR achieves a pooled PSNR gain of +0.040 dB, while PromptFix degrades all four pooled metrics under every prompt. These results show that recognizing shallow-water degradation does not imply reliable physical restoration and provide a reproducible baseline for future work.|[2611.09740](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09740)|
|**2026-11-09**|**A lightweight convolutional neural network for target detection in an edge environment**|Hughes Perreault et.al.|This paper proposes a novel, lightweight convolutional neural network (CNN) designed for edge device deployment. The CNN is developed to recognize and classify threats or targets of interest. The model is equipped with a real-time hotspot detection algorithm, which enables it to process information quickly and accurately, resulting in improved inference time of up to 20 fps and a detection range of more than 400 meters with typical thermal situational awareness sensors. The proposed CNN has been rigorously tested and validated to demonstrate its high accuracy, sensitivity, and low false positive rate, providing a reliable solution for edge devices to detect potential targets. Additionally, the lightweight design of the CNN allows for easy deployment, making it an ideal solution for extending the security bubble for edge devices. The proposed model can be used for a variety of applications, including surveillance, security, and object recognition.|[2611.09756](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09756)|
|**2026-11-09**|**AR-Edit: Training-Free Autoregressive Video Editing without Inversion**|Hovhannes Margaryan et.al.|Editing a video stream in real time, without access to future frames, is essential for interactive applications. Existing training-free video editing methods, however, assume full offline access to the entire sequence, while streaming approaches either rely on additional training or are limited to specific editing techniques. We show that this limitation can be removed by analyzing the self-attention dynamics of DMD-distilled autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models. We uncover a key asymmetry: queries and keys remain stable and aligned with their source counterparts across denoising timesteps, while values diverge and encode the evolving content. Inherited from the non-distilled bidirectional teacher, this asymmetry is strongest at high noise, where few-step streaming models operate. The resulting mechanism is simple: a single forward pass suffices to capture source identity in the value features, which can be reused throughout generation. Starting from pure Gaussian noise and injecting only these source values reconstructs the input with fidelity matching or surpassing inversion-based methods, effectively eliminating inversion. Based on this insight, we introduce AR-Edit, a training-free, inversion-free method that caches source values once and selectively injects them during denoising. Across multiple AR backbones, AR-Edit enables real-time streaming editing with superior source preservation and tonal fidelity at minimal overhead.|[2611.09795](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09795)|
|**2026-11-09**|**BEV3SR: Evidence-Aware 3D Spatial Reasoning from Video**|Fan Zhu et.al.|3D spatial reasoning from video remains challenging for VLMs because videos provide sequential, view-local observations rather than persistent metric scene records. Many questions must be answered in a query-specified perspective rather than the observed camera viewpoint, and prompting with a fixed set of RGB views may miss sparse or occluded visual cues. To address these gaps, we introduce BEV3SR, a training-free and backbone-agnostic framework for RGB-D videos with camera parameters. A query treated as requiring geometric evidence is handled by the Geometric Reasoner, which deterministically computes the answer using the metric BEV records in the query-specified reference frame. Otherwise, a query treated as requiring visual evidence is passed to the Visual Evidence Aligner, which uses BEV-indexed object records and their associated supporting views to retrieve object-consistent and scene-context RGB frames for VLM grounding. Within a standard 16-frame budget, BEV3SR consistently improves different VLM backbones across multiple 3D scene QA benchmarks, delivering large gains on spatially intensive questions and significant improvements on open-ended scene QA. These results demonstrate that BEV3SR offers an effective, efficient, and interpretable framework for explicit 3D spatial reasoning from video.|[2611.09816](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09816)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Health-System-Wide Mapping of Demographic Information in Routine Clinical Histopathology**|Shengjia Chen et.al.|The extent to which demographic-associated information is detectable across organs and disease states in routine clinical histopathology remains unclear. Here, we analyzed 1.3 million hematoxylin and eosin whole-slide images from 257,322 patients across 25 organs, using pathology foundation-model representations and multiple-instance learning to systematically map information associated with sex, age group, and self-reported race. Together, these findings provide a systematic map of demographic-associated information in routine histopathology, informing investigations of its sources, assessments of potential shortcut learning, and the development of more interpretable computational pathology models.|[2611.09851](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09851)|
|**2026-11-09**|**GRAC: Graph-Based Retrieval-Augmented Captioning for Surgical Scene Description**|Rochak Dhakal et.al.|Purpose: Surgical operative reports support procedural documentation, surgical training, performance assessment, and workflow review. However, extracting meaningful information often requires extensive manual review and clinical expertise. Automated surgical scene descriptions could reduce this burden by leveraging recurring surgical activities and prior knowledge. Methods: We propose GRAC (Graph-Based Retrieval-Augmented Captioning), a framework that represents surgical scenes as graphs capturing relationships among instruments, actions, and anatomical structures. Given an input scene graph, GRAC learns to retrieve relevant graph-caption pairs from training data. It compares instrument-tissue interactions between retrieved examples and the input scene, using comparison features to condition retrieved-caption representations during generation. Results: On the EndoVis-18 benchmark, GRAC achieved a CIDEr-D of 5.88, compared with 4.23 for SGT and 3.36 for the strongest conventional Transformer baseline. Exact instrument-action-target triplet-set accuracy reached 52.6%, compared with 32.7% for SGT and 26.2% for the strongest conventional baseline. These results demonstrate improvements in language quality and factual agreement. On TORS, GRAC achieved a CIDEr-D of 5.06, compared with 3.80 for the strongest baseline. Conclusion: GRAC demonstrates that combining structured surgical scene representations with retrieved examples improves surgical scene captioning and produces more contextually meaningful descriptions.|[2611.09866](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09866)|
|**2026-11-09**|**Graded convolutional networks: scale invariance without the scale and robustness to unknown blur**|Tanush Shaska et.al.|The Gaussian jet of an image is a graded vector space: a dilation by $\lambda$ multiplies derivatives of order $k$ by $\lambda^{-k}$. Local graded convolutions are exactly homogeneous differential operators, and a local feature is dilation-invariant if and only if it factors through the scale-normalized jet or, without the scale, through the retraction onto the weighted sphere of the grading. Under unknown Gaussian blur the retraction changes only by a change of scale, whereas the scale-normalized jet acquires a grading factor that no radial normalization removes. On MNIST such a network keeps 88.4% accuracy under a two-pixel blur, against 66.2% for the same network on scale-normalized derivatives; in Lindeberg's Gaussian derivative network the retraction raises accuracy at that blur from 91.5% to 95.1%.|[2611.09878](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09878)|
|**2026-11-09**|**ZeoBench: A Benchmark for Self-Supervised Learning on 3D Zeolite Representations**|Aaron Sun et.al.|Zeolites are an important class of porous crystalline materials with broad applications in gas storage, separations, and catalysis. Although hundreds of thousands of experimentally realized and hypothetical zeolite structures are known, identifying materials optimized for a target application remains challenging because property labels are expensive to obtain and available only for limited structure-molecule pairs. While prior work has demonstrated that 3D neural representations can predict zeolite properties effectively, it remains unclear which representation-learning strategies are most effective in the low-data regime relevant to materials discovery. Our study compares self-supervised 3D pretraining on volumetric zeolite data, adaptations of image-pretrained 2D ConvNets and vision transformers to 3D via multiview and channel-inflation strategies, and 3D ConvNets pretrained on out-of-domain volumetric datasets, alongside hand-crafted descriptors and supervised baselines. |**2026-11-09**|**FlowMoP: Stochastic Multi-Person Motion Prediction**|Aadya Agrawal et.al.|Predicting the future motion of multiple people is inherently stochastic: the same observed history admits many plausible continuations depending on intent, coordination, and social context. A B-spline reparameterization compresses the generative state space while enforcing temporal smoothness, and a dual-stream transformer conditions each agent's predictions on observed neighbor motion for interaction-aware forecasting. |**2026-11-09**|**Video-Coder: Reinforcing Agentic Coding for Video Generation**|Chaoyang Wang et.al.|Recent years have witnessed remarkable progress in video generation in terms of visual quality and temporal consistency. However, generating videos with precise spatial structure, coherent motion dynamics, and controllable scene composition remains challenging. In this paper, we introduce Video-Coder, the first attempt to train a code-driven video generation agent, which autonomously writes and executes code to construct a dynamic 3D scene (Draft Video) that provides structure and motion signals to downstream video generators. To support this paradigm, we design a dedicated data pipeline and curate two high-quality datasets, Video-Coder-SFT-6k and Video-Coder-RL-2k, which contain diverse prompts and ground-truth reference videos. We further develop the Video- Coder Harness, a unified interactive environment integrating code execution, 3D scene rendering, and execution feedback for agent training and inference. |**2026-11-09**|**Deep-Learning Algorithm for Indeterminate Thyroid Nodules: Comparison and Combination with Molecular Testing**|Jichen Yang et.al.|Thyroid nodules with indeterminate cytology (Bethesda III) carry an intermediate risk of malignancy, and molecular testing is widely used to decide between surgery and surveillance, but it is costly and not universally available. On the test set, the deep learning (DL) model achieved an AUC of 0.73 (95% CI 0.51-0.94), compared with 0.55 (0.38-0.71) for molecular testing (P = 0.15). At a cut-off inherited from the original algorithm, the DL model was more specific than molecular testing (0.95 vs 0.32; P < 0.001), with a higher positive predictive value (0.67 vs 0.21), a lower sensitivity (0.44 vs 0.78; P = 0.25) and a similar negative predictive value (0.88 vs 0.86). |**2026-11-09**|**Meituan-Robotics-0: A Vision-Language-Action Foundation Model for Desktop Manipulation**|Meituan Robotics Team et.al.|We present Meituan-Robotics-0 (MR0), a vision-language-action foundation model and embodied-agent system for stationary-base desktop manipulation. |**2026-11-09**|**Dynamic GPU Voxel Grids and Voxel-sequential Processing in NanoVDB**|Eftychios Sifakis et.al.|We introduce two fundamental advances to the NanoVDB data structure: dynamic GPU-native topological updates, and a voxel-sequential access paradigm that dramatically accelerates point-wise and stencil-based operators on sparse grids. NanoVDB's linearized memory layout has delivered strong GPU performance, and the Index-Grid concept introduced in the fVDB framework [Williams et al 2024] provided topology/data separation and optimal compactness of storage in sparse machine learning workloads. However, all these prior iterations of NanoVDB assume a static topology that has precluded use of this data structure in simulation and dynamic geometric processes. This achieves compute density and regularity that allow even complex stencil-based operators to approach peak memory bandwidth, with minimal storage overhead. |**2026-11-09**|**Measurement-Aware Multimodal LLM for No-Reference Low-Dose CT Quality Assessment**|Kerem Basim et.al.|No-reference image quality assessment (IQA) of low-dose CT predicts the score that radiologists would assign to a slice without a full-dose reference, as needed to compare dose protocols and denoisers. |**2026-11-09**|**On the Few-Shot Adaptation of Vision-Language Models: A Few Gradient Signs Are All You Need**|Nairouz Mrabah et.al.|The limited supervision available for the few-shot adaptation of vision-language models makes gradient estimation particularly noisy. This motivates SignSparseSGD and DynSignSparseSGD, two state-free optimizers that use gradient magnitudes only to select where to update and fixed-magnitude signs to decide how to update. |**2026-11-09**|**Global Solar Siting from Geospatial Embeddings**|Abdullah Rehman et.al.|Global solar photovoltaic (PV) capacity is rapidly growing, helping decrease our emissions and dependence on fossil fuels. |**2026-11-09**|**Segmenting Geometric Textures of 3D Meshes**|Weidan Xiong et.al.|Recognizing geometric texture patterns on 3D models is critical for applications such as heritage protection, model editing, and 3D printing. |**2026-11-09**|**Flex-RSI: Benchmarking Recursive Self-Improvement of Frontier Agents**|Jingdong Zhang et.al.|Frontier language models are increasingly deployed as agents, and how well an agent performs depends not only on its model but also on its harness: the prompts, tools, memory, and control loop through which the model acts. |**2026-11-09**|**UniMore: Fine-Grained Supervision for Unified Understanding and Generation**|Haoyue Bai et.al.|Unified multimodal models combine image understanding and generation within one system, creating an opportunity to improve both capabilities through paired image--text supervision. We examine the outputs of a pretrained unified model in both directions and observe a loss of specificity: generated images can contain the requested objects with incorrect attributes, while captions identify a scene without expressing its distinguishing details. |**2026-11-09**|**DobotTrack: Target-Grounded Vision-Language-Action Learning for Embodied Person Tracking**|Shengjun Deng et.al.|Language-guided embodied person tracking requires a mobile agent to identify a target from a referring description and consistently follow the target under egocentric observations. |**2026-11-09**|**EventHOI: Event-Driven Resource-Efficient Hand-Object Interaction Logging on Smartglasses**|Le Zhang et.al.|Hand-object interactions (HOIs) can be captured by smartglasses to learn how people manipulate objects in daily life. |**2026-11-09**|**Semantic Extraction Framework for Goal-Oriented V2X Communication Supporting Safe Intersection Passage**|Haoran Yang et.al.|This paper proposes a transmitter-side semantic extraction framework for goal-oriented Vehicle-to-Everything (V2X) communication supporting safe intersection passage. |**2026-11-09**|**SIFR: Stable Signed Flow Reinforcement**|Runlong Liao et.al.|Reward-based fine-tuning of flow models requires effectively promoting high-reward and suppressing low-reward samples, while maintaining training stability over many rounds of iterative updates. |**2026-11-09**|**A Stochastic Latent Variable Generalization of Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures**|Nikos Giakoumoglou et.al.|Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) predict the latent embedding of a target signal from a context signal, with an auxiliary variable z conditioning the predictor. |**2026-11-09**|**Encoding Memory in World Models with Test-Time Model-Based Reinforcement Learning**|Maxime Burchi et.al.|Model-based reinforcement learning (MBRL) approaches have demonstrated strong performance across diverse domains in recent years, learning accurate world models from agent trajectories. |**2026-11-09**|**Beyond Segmentation: Enriching ImageCAS with Coronary Ostia Landmarks, Rooted Centerline Graphs, and Open-Weight Localization Models**|Matteo Leccardi et.al.|Coronary ostia and centerlines are key reference points for automated analysis of coronary computed tomography angiography (CCTA), from surgical planning to intraoperative vessel tracking. |**2026-11-09**|**Multiscale Closing Index (MCI) for measuring structural similarity between 2D point sets**|Nicol\`o Zava et.al.|We introduce Multi-scale Closing Index (MCI), a new dissimilarity measure for evaluating the alignment of two 2D point sets. |**2026-11-09**|**AnimWeb-Bench: Benchmarking Animated Webpage Code Generation from Videos**|Zhongyi Zhang et.al.|Animation is pervasive on the Web, the CSS animation property alone appears on over three-quarters of webpages. |**2026-11-09**|**ROSE: Risk-Aware Orthogonal Subspace Navigation for Lifelong Knowledge Editing in Multimodal Large Language Models**|Lingyun Song et.al.|Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) require continuous factual updates to stay current, yet lifelong knowledge editing remains a daunting challenge. |**2026-11-09**|**Seeing What Matters: Benchmarking Task-Conditioned Actionable Spatial Belief for Manipulation**|Yina Jian et.al.|When should a robot look at the cup, and when at the portafilter?
